#2768. Tech world's calls for Jensen Huang to return to Nvidia are growing

An online petition calling for Jensen Huang's reinstatement as CEO of Nvidia has drawn more than 160,000 signatures, as prominent tech executives, employees and shareholders rally behind the chipmaker's co-founder following his resignation last week.

The "Stand with Jensen" campaign has attracted support from Tesla CEO Elon Musk, Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney and Y Combinator CEO Garry Tan, according to the petition's website, which notes that affiliations are self-reported and not verified.

Huang, who co-founded the company in 1993 and helped turn it into a dominant supplier of artificial intelligence chips, resigned as Nvidia's president, CEO and board member last week following reports of extramarital affairs — including one involving a woman suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence.

Nvidia appointed longtime finance chief Colette Kress as interim CEO and said its board would consider both internal and external candidates in its search for a permanent successor. The growing campaign to reinstate Huang adds a layer of complexity to the decision for Nvidia's board as it looks to put the controversy behind it, and coincides with reports that he is considering a comeback.

Any potential return, however, faces significant hurdles. Nvidia's board is continuing an independent investigation into the conduct that led to Huang's departure. What's more, U.S. lawmakers are preparing for investigations into how the now-ex-CEO's affair may have damaged America's artificial intelligence dominance, the national security implications and the executive's close relationship with President Donald Trump and his Cabinet.

Musk was among the most prominent tech executives to publicly back Huang's reinstatement, saying on X that he would sign a petition calling for the former Nvidia chief's return.

"A petition is underway to reinstate Jensen and I'm signing it too," Musk wrote on X. He had previously also said it would be "an honor" to hire Huang.

The campaign has drawn support from people identifying themselves as current and former Nvidia employees, shareholders and business partners, according to the petition's website. It calls on Nvidia's board to ensure a fair investigation into Huang's conduct and provide greater transparency around the circumstances of his departure.

Some Nvidia employees have also publicly expressed support for Huang, with one engineer saying he had decided to leave the company following the CEO's departure.

Darrell Dai, who identified himself as a robotics engineer at Nvidia, said Huang's vision and the engineering culture he fostered were key reasons he had stayed at the company.

Another Nvidia employee, Vartika Singh, who said she had worked at the chipmaker for nearly eight years, praised Huang's leadership and personal support during a family medical crisis.

"I have never worked for anyone quite like Jensen Huang, and I doubt I ever will," Singh wrote on X, describing his commitment to employees, researchers and startups.

The push has also gained traction among major shareholders, with Altimeter Capital CEO Brad Gerstner and Atreides Management's Gavin Baker urging Nvidia's board to consider all options, including Huang's potential return, in an open letter Friday.

The investors called for a measured approach to choosing Nvidia's next leader, arguing that Huang deserved a fair process before the board made any permanent decision.

Huang has discussed the possibility of returning as Nvidia's chief executive with close confidants, the Financial Times reported, citing multiple people familiar with the matter.

One of his allies told the FT that supporters were assembling a "war room" of Silicon Valley executives to lobby for his reinstatement and would seek backing from the White House.

The efforts had Huang's support, the person added, according to the report.

"We are in the process of selecting a search firm for the NVIDIA CEO role. We have not formally engaged any potential candidates," an Nvidia spokesperson said in response to CNBC's request for comment on the company's search.

The spokesperson added that any speculation about individual candidates, whether internal or external, was premature.

Anthony Scaramucci, founder of alternative investment firm SkyBridge and a former White House communications director, also dismissed the prospect of Huang returning as CEO, saying the investigations surrounding him presented obstacles that a public campaign could not overcome.

"The tech bros want Jensen back. Not happening," Scaramucci wrote on X, arguing that Nvidia's board could not reinstate a chief executive whose conduct remained under investigation.

In a separate post, Scaramucci rejected comparisons with OpenAI CEO Sam Altman, who returned to the company days after being ousted in 2023. "The FBI doesn't take petitions," he wrote, saying that Huang's situation was fundamentally different from Altman's. Altman's ouster stemmed from an internal governance dispute, while Huang's situation involves ongoing investigations into his conduct that make a swift board reversal far more difficult.

Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company linked to the controversy, said recent media reports contained assertions about its business and supply chain that were not supported by verifiable evidence.

The company said it had retained external legal counsel to conduct an internal investigation and that Alice Huang, a woman reportedly romantically linked to Jensen Huang and an alleged Chinese spy, left Megaspeed effective Aug. 30, 2025, and no longer holds any role there.

Alice Huang may have owned stakes in businesses in China that benefited Nvidia's biggest competitor in China, Huawei, CNBC reported earlier.

Meanwhile, Nvidia's board has approached Arm CEO Rene Haas as a potential successor to Huang, the FT reported. Nvidia's board said at the time that it had not formally engaged any candidates.

Some analysts see a path for Huang to return despite the controversy surrounding his departure.

Jay Goldberg, a senior analyst at Seaport Research Partners, said Huang could make a comeback if he overcomes his legal challenges, citing his influence in the AI industry and continued support from investors.

"If he can get through the legal trouble, I think we'll have a second act from him," Goldberg told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia."

But not everyone on Wall Street is convinced it can happen so soon.

"We see no chance Jensen comes back to Nvidia as CEO," Dan Ives, partner and senior managing director at Yorkville Ives & Co., said in a note.

Ives said the shareholder petition and public pressure could create further distractions for Nvidia, but would not change the central task facing interim CEO Kress: restoring stability and investor confidence.

Ives added that Nvidia's leadership in AI chips and its long-term demand outlook remained intact despite the management upheaval.

Nvidia is scheduled to report its fiscal third-quarter earnings Tuesday, putting Kress and the company's leadership team under the spotlight as investors look for reassurance about its future.

#2766. Dak Prescott: 49ers fans "were more present than ours"

On Sunday, America's Team wasn't even Dallas's Team. 49ers fans showed up in big numbers for the game against the Cowboys at AT&T Stadium.

After the game, Cowboys quarterback Dak Prescott was asked whether the home team had to use the silent count.

"No, we didn't go on the silent count," Prescott said. "[We] anticipated it. I think y'all even potentially asked me on Thursday about it, having this many 49er fans there. Tricked myself and told myself early on that the red was for my high school.

"And at the end of the day, all I asked for is a great environment to play this game. And unfortunately, their fans were more present than ours, but it created a great atmosphere. So, we take the good out of it one way or another."

For owner Jerry Jones, the colors worn by the fans don't matter; their money is the same shade of green.

Still, it's always a bad look when too many fans of the opposing team show up for a road game. On Sunday, 49ers fans found their way to buy tickets and infiltrate the venue.

And their fans got to see the Niners move to 8-1 on the season.

#2765. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2764. Thunder Get Back on Track with Dominant Win Over Kings

The Oklahoma City Thunder got back on track Sunday night, snapping a three-game losing streak with a much-needed 125-103 victory over the Sacramento Kings. That type of skid is not something this team has faced in several years, and with all the injuries and misfortune Oklahoma City has experienced this season, every win is going to matter if the Thunder is going to stay afloat and make the playoffs.

Oklahoma City dominated this one, getting off to a strong start in the first quarter and building an early 13-4 lead. Although Sacramento did a good job of battling back and eventually tying the game, the Thunder threw another counterpunch and ran away with it.

Shai Gilgeous-Alexander was the star of the show with an ultra-efficient night, scoring 37 points in just 30 minutes. This comes on the heels of scoring 40 points against the LA Clippers on Friday night, as he continues to put on a show on the offensive end.

Outside of him, Aday Mara was a huge spark for Oklahoma City. The rookie center finished with 16 points, 13 rebounds and three assists. Although he isn't getting enough attention nationally, he's been one of the best rookies in the entire NBA to this point in the season. He's had to step up and play significant minutes despite being a first-year player on a team with high expectations, and he's risen to the challenge.

Ajay Mitchell also returned from injury in this one, and although he didn't have his best game, the attention he commands from opposing defenses opens things up for Gilgeous-Alexander, which certainly could have contributed to his efficient night. Isaiah Hartenstein pulled down 11 rebounds and Kenrich Williams chipped in 10 points.

But what really made this game different from some of Oklahoma City's recent contests was the defensive side of the ball. The Thunder did what it's most known for, which is generating turnovers and scoring off them. That hasn't been the case nearly as much this season with all the injuries, but Oklahoma City forced 21 turnovers on Sunday night.

The Kings are, of course, one of the worst teams in the NBA this season, and this was the type of bounce-back opportunity Oklahoma City really needed on the schedule. That doesn't mean it was a free win, though. The Thunder still had to show up and take care of business, which is exactly what it did to get back on track.

#2761. Sixers, Brown have a ‘humbling' night in loss to Celtics

Sixers, Brown have a 'humbling' night in loss to Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Whatever Jaylen Brown and the Sixers dreamed up for their first matchup against the Celtics soon flew out the window on Tuesday night.

Brown was whistled for two early fouls, the Sixers fell behind and their early-season flaws were on full display in a 123-101 loss at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

"We've just got to get better," Brown said. "Tonight was humbling. We got outplayed, out-executed. So we go back to the drawing board and get ready for the next one."

Over 26 minutes, Brown had 13 points on 4-for-9 shooting and five rebounds against the team that traded him away this summer.

As for the endless dissection of his relationship with former co-star Jayson Tatum, Brown didn't have much to add Tuesday. There was no public reconciliation, no obvious on-court animosity and generally nothing to satisfy fans hoping for some kind of fireworks on that front.

"I'm just focused on doing what's best for my team," Brown said. "Everything else … to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson; he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics. They executed, they shot the hell out of the ball and they were the better team tonight."

Tatum scored 21 points, two of which came on a third-quarter slam over LeBron James. Paul George continued his fantastic start as a Celtic by dropping 36 points and draining six three-pointers.

The Celtics dominated from beyond the arc, shooting 22 for 49 (44.9 percent). The Sixers went just 11 for 37 (29.7 percent) on another night of underwhelming offense. The team sits 22nd in the NBA in offensive rating, 24th in three-point makes per game and 21st in three-point percentage.

"I think that we've got some guys that are obviously shooting much lower percentages than their last season or their career average — almost a handful of guys," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "And I don't think it's because they're shooting fallaway, totally contested ones. I think they're shots we want them to take. So I think they'll continue to take 'em and I'm pretty sure those will even out into their career averages."

Joel Embiid is at 37 percent from the floor and 4 for 33 from three-point range (12.1 percent).

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center got through an injury scare when he rolled his ankle late in the second quarter and hit a three early in the third. It was his first long-range make since the Sixers' home opener back on Oct. 22.

"It's a work in progress," Embiid said. "I think the biggest thing is just not thinking. I'm probably overthinking it. The rhythm of the game is different. I'm so used to having to create for myself, so it's a little bit different.

"But I think I'm slowly getting better. You could see as soon as I made a three, the next shot in the short corner … I didn't even think about it. I just shot it and let it fly, and I made it. … I think it's just about taking what's in front of you. Make or miss, keep working on your game. It's going to get there at some point."

Even with the Sixers second in the Eastern Conference standings at 9-3, Embiid believes the team's defense has "slacked off a little bit" in recent games.

"I don't really look at the record," he said. "I just look at the process of, 'Are we getting better every day?' We can always do a better job at all phases of the game."

"They're one of the top teams in the league and they've been together for a while," Brown said. "We've got a lot to work on over here, so we've got to get to work."

The Sixers will face the Raptors on Thursday night in Philadelphia.

They'll have to wait until Jan. 21 in Boston to see the Celtics again.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said. "I was looking forward to playing well, but s— happens. Just get ready for the next one."

#2759. Marlins Must Avoid Their Biggest Setback of 2026

The Miami Marlins can take several lessons from the 2026 season, as many factors led them to fall short of a playoff berth. But as with any team in Major League Baseball, the Fish (80-82) had a solid run that wasn't able to withstand losing a chunk of time in the race.

It's been the downfall of many teams who, like the Marlins, were left hanging by a fingernail only to fall out of contention thanks to a long losing streak. And for a club that looked like it was well on its way to grabbing a ticket to postseason paradise.

The dreaded 12-game losing streak in July will go down in the Marlins' history as the single biggest factor in why this team failed to qualify. And quite frankly, the entire debacle left manager Clayton McCullough and president of baseball operations Peter Bendix with a lot of egg on their faces.

The problem for Miami wasn't simply that it lost 12 consecutive games. It was the timing of those losses and the way they completely changed the complexion of a season that had suddenly become much more promising than anyone anticipated.

Coming off a remarkable 20-6 June, the Marlins had positioned themselves as legitimate contenders in the National League Wild Card race. They were 10 games above .500 when the skid began, had momentum on their side, and were playing with the kind of confidence that can carry a young team into October. Suddenly, all of that disappeared.

Twelve games might represent only a small fraction of a 162-game schedule, but the damage was enormous. Instead of building on their June success and putting additional pressure on the teams ahead of them, Miami spent nearly two weeks searching for answers, watching its position in the standings deteriorate with every passing night.

Baseball is a marathon, and every club experiences difficult stretches. Even eventual playoff teams encounter losing streaks, injuries, offensive slumps, and stretches when nothing seems to go right. The difference is that successful teams generally find ways to limit the damage. The Marlins didn't.

Those 12 defeats effectively erased much of the ground Miami had gained during its outstanding June. More importantly, they forced the club into a position where it had very little margin for error during the remaining months of the season. Every subsequent series carried additional importance, and every missed opportunity became another obstacle in an increasingly difficult climb.

There were opportunities to recover. The Marlins showed enough resilience to remain competitive and eventually finish with an 80-82 record, just two games below .500. That final mark is particularly frustrating when considering how far this team had come and how much potential it demonstrated during the summer.

Had Miami managed even a modest split during that 12-game implosion, the postseason conversation might have looked considerably different. A winning record over those games could have put the club in an entirely different position entering August.

Instead, the Marlins spent the rest of the season trying to make up for a disastrous July that ultimately proved too costly.

For McCullough and Bendix, the streak should serve as a defining lesson heading into the offseason. Developing young talent and building a sustainable contender are important objectives, but learning how to navigate adversity is equally critical.

The 2026 Marlins didn't necessarily lack the talent to make the playoffs. They lacked the consistency to finish the job. And for a team that came so close to turning an unexpected summer surge into a postseason appearance, those 12 games will remain the most painful reminder of what might have been.

Kirk Cousins. It wasn't an outstanding stat line from Cousins in his first start since the Vikings traded for him, but it was more than competent with some key passes that turned into touchdowns while allowing the Vikings to win the time of possession battle with intelligent play despite just two full practices since arriving in Minnesota. Jakobe Thomas. He got his first reps of the season on special teams against the Packers and forced a fumble which Jake Golday recovered and put the game out of reach.

Jay Ward. He had a personal foul early in the game on special teams after some extracurricular activity that wasn't smart. Can't give away cheap yards like that, especially on the road.

The Minnesota Vikings as legitimate Super Bowl contenders with Cousins at quarterback and perhaps the most dominant defense in recent years.

#2728. The Colts playing Daniel Jones is indefensible

Why? Just why? Why is Daniel Jones out there for the Indianapolis Colts? It literally doesn't get any worse, so maybe that's the reason. Maybe it's the $50 million Chris Ballard gave him this year. Maybe he has something on Shane Steichen? I don't know what it is. All I know is Jones shouldn't see the field again this year.

Jones started his career with the Colts in the most incredible fashion. It is ending that way too. Incredibly. It is incredible how he is playing right now. A 1.7 QBR is the worst of any starting quarterback this year. That's worse than Baker Mayfield completing zero passes. That's worse than Carson Wentz on Monday Night Football against the Bills. Both of those performances were dreadful statistically. Jones beat them both.

This wasn't against the Vikings' defense. This wasn't him trying to keep pace with the best offense in the league. This was against the 1-7 Dolphins who ran a quarterback out there for his first start. Jones threw two picks and had 108 yards. Not all picks are fully on the quarterback, but the one returned for a touchdown could have been thrown better. Everything this year for Jones could be better. Not that the options to replace him are glowing, but Shane Steichen sticking with him at this point is insanity. Nothing tells you he is the guy.

Wanting a change at quarterback isn't just whiny fandom. It is a legitimate concern. Daniel Jones was horrid against one of the worst teams in the league. Things don't get any easier than a home game against the Dolphins. Yes, talent has been depleted due to trades, but Jones has to be better. If he can't be, then he needs to be done. The Colts have to make the change.

#2719. Browns get another fourth-quarter comeback, defeat Texans 27-24

With another late, fourth-quarter comeback, Cleveland defeated Houston 27-24 for the club’s sixth win of the season.

The Browns got down 17-0 early, but scored 20 straight points to take a 20-17 lead late in the third quarter.

But that disappeared in the fourth period, when Houston went down the field and scored on an 11-yard touchdown pass after a missed field goal Andre Szmyt. The Texans were aided by a key defensive pass interference penalty on the possession.

Cleveland had multiple opportunities to answer and finally did on the third try. A 30-yard pass from Deshaun Watson to Harold Fannin put the Browns in scoring position at the 5-yard line. Then on the next play, Quinshon Judkins punched it in for a 5-yard, go-ahead score to put the Browns up by three.

The Texans had a chance to tie it up with a field goal, getting the ball back with 47 seconds on the clock. But after getting a first down, Houston couldn’t move the ball any further and C.J. Stroud’s final fourth-down pass fell incomplete for a turnover on downs.

Houston scored just once after putting up 17 points on its first three drives. Stroud finished the game 20-of-38 for 211 yards with three touchdowns and one interception. Nico Collins had nine catches for 94 yards with a TD.

On the other side, Watson completed 25-of-40 passes for 312 yards with a touchdown. He was the team’s leading rusher with 49 yards on eight carries with a TD. Fannin led with 91 yards on seven catches.

With the win, the Browns head into their Week 11 bye at 6-4. As noted on the Fox broadcast, Cleveland is now 4-0 at home for the first time since 1963. They will next host the Raiders in Week 12.

The 3-6 Texans have a short week upcoming, as they’ll play the Colts on Thursday night.

#2717. LeBron James climbs to third on NBA all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd

The NBA icon passed Jason Kidd to move into third for most assists all time in pro basketball. He did so during the Philadelphia 76ers 131-124 win over the Indiana Pacers on Sunday, Nov. 15 at Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia.

James' 12,092nd assist came with 1:19 left in the fourth quarter. Dean Wade passed the ball to James, who was standing near the free throw line. James quickly tossed the ball up to VJ Edgecombe, who took two steps under the basket and slammed a dunk.

James spoke on how passing the ball has been a foundational part of his game from the beginning. He is now playing in his 24th season, an NBA record.

"From the moment I picked up a basketball when I first started playing like recreational basketball, five-on-five, I always got a joy and a kick out of seeing my teammates score," he said on the NBC Sports broadcast after the win. "It was a feeling that felt really good about me being able to help one of my teammates out. ... Yeah, I got a kick out of that from day one."

James needs 461 assists to pass Chris Paul, who sits in second place on the assists list with 12,552. John Stockton holds the NBA record with 15,806 assists. James is already the NBA's all-time leading points scorer.

"It means I had a lot of great teammates," James said of reaching the assists mark. "You gotta have teammates that can put the ball in the hole. My job is just try to put it on time, on target. Lead 'em to the hole either from layups, dunks, threes, midrange, whatever the case may be.

"Any time I'm on any list in NBA history, I love the history of the game. I love the players that came before me. It's super honorable."

The landmark assist was one of 11 that James had on the night. He added 15 points and seven rebounds to his stat line. Jaylen Brown led Philadelphia with 31 points, two rebounds and two assists. Tyrese Maxey added 27 points, four rebounds and six assists.

Pascal Siakam had 31 points, two rebounds and two assists for Indiana in the loss.

James joined the 76ers this offseason after leaving the Los Angeles Lakers. In his time in Philadelphia so far this season, James beat the defending champion New York Knicks in the season opener and helped with a 29-point comeback against his hometown team, the Cleveland Cavaliers.

#2715. Wales must look to back up All Blacks win - Tandy

Wales head coach Steve Tandy has challenged his side to back up their wonderful win against New Zealand when they host Australia next weekend.

After beating the All Blacks for the first time in 73 years with the 35-28 win at the Principality Stadium in Cardiff, Wales face the final game of the Nations Championship - before the play-offs - against the Wallabies on Saturday evening (20:10 BST).

Tandy says Wales will attempt not to dwell on what they achieved on Saturday against New Zealand as they aim for a third successive home win.

"We have to keep going and we've got a massive game against Australia next weekend," said Tandy.

"It's going to be a different kind of test for us after we have had back-to-back home wins.

"We've gone up another notch or another couple of levels, so it's important we back that up. It's about making sure when we come in that we're ready to work."

After defeat to England, Australia lost 15-12 against Scotland on Sunday, a result which leaves Wales fifth in the northern hemisphere table.

Wales are behind behind Gregor Townsend's side on points difference with Scotland finishing their campaign against Japan.

Tandy's side have won three out of five games in the Nations Championship against Fiji, Japan and New Zealand with defeats in the summer against Argentina and South Africa.

Captain Dewi Lake has highlighted what has changed for this campaign to enable Wales to defeat New Zealand and rise one place in the world rankings to ninth.

"Our skills are better and I think we're a better team which is credit to Steve and his coaches," said Lake.

"I think the belief is also different and the mindset has shifted.

"We've spoken about it a lot and it's probably easy sometimes to say we've changed our mindset and believe in ourselves.

"It feels different. Everybody has come in with a voice and a belief and are challenging each other to get better. Ultimately that's what grows a group quickly."

"They're young men and we want these players to go out there and play with freedom.

#2714. Winners and Losers from Seahawks vs. Raiders: It’s JSN. It’s always JSN.

The Seattle Seahawks had 30 minutes to arguably save their season after falling behind 10-7 to the Las Vegas Raiders at halftime. Given the deep nature of the NFC playoff picture, Seattle had little margin for error to lose too many more games, and especially not versus inferior (on paper) opposition. Mike Macdonald's team locked in after halftime and got the win they needed to stay afloat in the playoff race heading into their bye week.

Let's get to Winners and Losers from Seahawks 21, Raiders 13!

You can take a TD away from JSN through a Super Bowl XL-esque "pushoff" penalty, but you can't hold him down forever. JSN caught 8 passes for 171 yards and a 71-yard TD, and that's with two explosive plays wiped out due to penalty and a drop. He could've easily broken 200 without much effort, which is ridiculous regardless of opposition.

JSN is on pace for a 2,000-yard season and he has the Triple Crown lead over the rest of the NFL in yards, receptions, and touchdowns. When you're the best in the business in a deep field of elite receivers, you're special. JSN is one of a kind.

Cooper Kupp does something he's never done before

It's fitting that Seattle's two slot machines scored touchdowns in Las Vegas. Kupp's lone catch was a sliding grab for 34 yards on a scramble drill, marking the first time he's ever caught a touchdown from a backup quarterback in his NFL career. That sounds nerdy and is partially down to the durability of Matthew Stafford, Jared Goff, and (well, at least until this season) Sam Darnold, but even great WRs occasionally get a garbage time TD from the backup.

For the first time in his career, Jadarian Price is an NFL touchdown scorer. He had nine carries for 36 yards, showcasing his speed on his 12-yard touchdown run. Price also had a big kick return to start the game, although it was wasted on an opening drive fumble. There are ways for Price to get his touches without being the lead back, and he adds another splash play to his reel in the form of a TD.

Zach Charbonnet gets some hard-earned yards

The blocking wasn't always there, but Charbonnet was on the cusp of his first 100-yard game since returning from injury. He had 94 yards on 20 carries, with several Raiders defenders left in the dust after he chucked them off of him. What sets him apart from Jadarian Price is his ability to drive forward after first contact more effectively than the rookie, and that's one of the reason he'll be RB1 moving forward.

Four games played, four wins for Drew Lock. In fact, when you add in his two starts in 2023 he has a 4-1 record as a starter and an unofficial 5-1 record as Seahawks QB. Lock was 14/17 for 240 yards and 2 TDs, and his numbers would've been better if not for a couple of negated plays via penalty. The first fumble was sloppy and not a smart play when a sack is totally fine, but the second fumble was unlucky. His biggest moment was lowering the shoulder and calling game on a bootleg run in the final minutes.

Whether Lock starts after the bye or not, the important thing is that he's comfortable in the offense and taking advantage of defenses he should be taking advantage of. He's going to get significant money this offseason, whether it's Seattle believing he might be the new long-term answer or some other team that sees Drew as a high-end bridge or "the next Sam Darnold" of sorts. This is the best Lock has ever been arguably in his entire career, irrespective of opponent.

So what if Spoon doesn't get interceptions? When you have a cornerback who can play slot or outside, defend the run as well as defend the pass, and blitz quarterbacks better than most linebackers, you've got yourself one hell of a chess piece. Witherspoon sacked Fernando Mendoza twice and is a total menace when anything is near the line of scrimmage.

Big Murph has five sacks on the season, which leads the Seahawks. He wanted to reach 10 in his third year and he's halfway there. Murphy's run defense hasn't always been strong this season, but he continues leveling up as a pass rusher.

Dante Fowler picked up his second sack in as many weeks, coming in unblocked on a blitz when Nick Emmanwori attacked the inside. DeMarcus Lawrence and Derick Hall both had sacks and several pressures of Fernando Mendoza. Fowler having any sort of positive contribution is welcome given his underwhelming start to the season, and if he's going to keep having a low snap count then he might as well maximize his opportunities.

Seattle's secondary blankets Raiders' outmatched receivers

The Raiders don't have a quality wide receiver group, with all due respect to Cody White and his one catch for 22 yards. When targeting wide receivers, Mendoza was 5/14 for 40 yards. Josh Jobe in particular had a fantastic performance and chipped in with two TFLs.

Tory Horton's 5D chess is part genius, part athletic brilliance

For reasons already stated in this article, Horton basically cost the Raiders their two-minute warning timeout by saving a touchback. He channeled his inner Richard Sherman with "The Tip" to Rashid Shaheed, albeit one that's an illegal bat by rule, and there went the Raiders' ideal outcome. That was impressive.

Bobby Hart gets the job done with Abe Lucas out

I was a little worried when Bobby Hart had to sub in for Abe Lucas after his ankle injury. Hart's last season starting with the Los Angeles Chargers was… brutal. Hart didn't give up any pressures that I saw. If you can sub in and have to deal with Maxx Crosby and not be a disaster, that's a win.

Myers has missed at least a field goal or an extra point in four of his last seven games, and it's not like 44 yards in an indoor facility is a difficult kick. I'm a little bit antsy with Myers and how he's performed recently and particularly a contract year.

The Seahawks were so injured that even the team doctor got hurt

Abe Lucas, George Holani, and Nick Kallerup all left and didn't return due to their respective injuries. Nick Emmanwori re-aggravated a shoulder injury, although he says he's all good. Jarran Reed left with a toe injury and later returned despite an initial doubtful designation. Even Seattle's team doctor then got whacked on the head by a wayward Fernando Mendoza throw, although thankfully he's alright.

Gaining Quenton Nelson, Charles Cross, Sam Darnold, and (hopefully) Leonard Williams after the bye may be offset by what happened on Sunday. Hopefully the bye week is an ideal one-week buffer for any players who only have short-term injuries.

Mike Macdonald ratcheted up the pressure once the first couple of Raiders drives finished. Nothing can get a rookie's clock sped up faster than dealing with all sorts of rushers coming right at him.

The playing surface was unacceptable. Rolling in a natural grass surface for an indoor environment (or quasi-indoor like the Arizona Cardinals stadium) is not new but that was no different than playing at a random city park. So much slipping and sliding and uneven footing made it such a nervy game to watch.

Don't overlook Rashid Shaheed's 3rd down conversion. His only catch kept Seattle's opening second half drive alive and resulted in an eventual touchdown. Shaheed was also wide open on JSN's TD but Lock turned him down for a bigger play.

Brian Fleury called a much better 2nd half than 1st half. And what I mean is he didn't make literally all but one first down playcall a pass like he did in the first 30 minutes. That was wild to see Seattle get overly obsessed with establishing the run to the point where Drew Lock had five pass attempts by halftime. That naked bootleg run by Lock was arguably the best Fleury call to date, and the offense lit up the Raiders in the third quarter. No, he's not getting fired at the bye week.

Seattle didn't get a ton of help in the playoff chase, but Week 12 just became as high leverage as can be. The Seahawks have a must-win against the San Francisco 49ers, which could potentially put them back within a game of the division lead if the 49ers lose to the Minnesota Vikings next Sunday. But the other aspect of Week 12 is the Green Bay Packers (6-4) playing the Los Angeles Rams (6-4) on Thanksgiving Eve, and the outcome of that game essentially determines Seattle's pecking order in the playoff hunt entering December. Seattle still hasn't played its best football of the season (at least I don't think), so get healthy during the bye and figure out how to attack this stretch run. At worst, I think Seattle needs to be 11-6 to reach the postseason. It's doable, but the Super Bowl champs have to find a way to get back to playing complementary football again regardless of who the starting QB is.

#2711. Sharks beat Predators 4-2 to end Nashville's 7-game win streak

SAN JOSE, Calif. (AP) — Macklin Celebrini and Will Smith each had a goal and two assists as the San Jose Sharks snapped the Nashville Predators' seven-game win streak, 4-2 on Sunday night.

Ivar Stenberg had a goal and an assist and Collin Graf also scored for the Sharks, while Alex Nedeljkovic made 32 saves.

Nils Hoglander and Steven Stamkos scored for the Predators and Juuse Saros stopped 20 shots.

Smith opened the scoring with 7:35 left in the first period with his eighth goal of the season after a breakaway that left him all alone after Stenberg found him.

Graf made it 2-0 with 3:50 left in the first period with his third goal of the season and first in 11 games.

Stenberg made it 3-0 at 4:45 of the second period with his third goal of the season, as Smith found him right in front for a tap-in while Saros had lost his stick.

Nashville got on the board late in the second period when Hoglander scored his fifth of the season after San Jose wasn't able to clear the puck. Adam Wilsby had the assist.

Celebrini made it 4-1 at 7:40 of the third period, with assists from Smith and defenseman Jacob Trouba.

Nashville cut the lead to 4-2 with 3:25 left to play on a power-play goal by Stamkos, his 12th of the season. The puck just sneaked inside the post and the goal was upheld after a review.

The Sharks have yet to record a shutout this season.

Predators: Visit the Minnesota Wild on Tuesday night.

Sharks: Host the Calgary Flames on Tuesday night.

BOSTON — The Celtics will start Derrick White, Jordan Walsh, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, and Neemias Queta when they face the Dallas Mavericks on Sunday night.

#2707. Texans lead Browns 17-10 at halftime

The Texans got out to a 17-0 lead in Cleveland, but the Browns have made it a game with 10 straight points.

Houston scored on each of its first three possessions, getting a 12-yard touchdown from C.J. Stroud to Nico Collins, a 45-yard field goal, and an 11-yard touchdown pass from Stroud to Xavier Hutchinson.

The Texans will receive the second-half kickoff.

#2706. Purdy, McCaffrey lead 49ers offense in win vs Cowboys: winners, losers

The San Francisco 49ers offense was just about unstoppable against the Dallas Cowboys in their Week 10 matchup.

The Niners put up 584 yards against the Cowboys on the way to a 46-40 win on Nov. 15 at AT&T Stadium in Arlington, Texas.

San Francisco quarterback Brock Purdy had another MVP-like performance, throwing for a career-high 389 yards, completing 22-of-28 passes for three touchdowns. But he wasn't the only one to notch a career-high. Niners running back Christian McCaffrey had his best day as a receiver catching four passes for a 161 yards (career-high) and two touchdowns. Deebo Samuel Sr. had 109 total yards and two touchdowns on the day.

The Cowboys defense pulled a no-show, but their offense wasn't too shabby. Dallas gained 439 total yards on offense as quarterback Dak Prescott tossed five touchdowns on 30-for-51 passes for 343 yards. Most of their damage was caused by George Pickens, who went off for 11 catches, 145 yards and three touchdowns.

The 49ers defense fended off the Cowboys when it mattered to advance to 8-1 on the season. Their Week 11 matchup is against the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City on Nov. 22, while the Cowboys face the the Tennessee Titans ahead of their annual Thanksgiving Day game.

49ers quarterback continues to have a scorching-hot 2026 season, leading San Francisco to the best record in the NFC. Against the Cowboys, in his first appearance at AT&T Stadium, Purdy didn't disappoint. Matter of fact, he went out for a career-high in passing yards with 389 in the win. He remains undefeated against the Cowboys, now 4-0, including the postseason.

Purdy put some good clips on the tape against the Cowboys. One play he danced in the pocket before escaping, extending the play, to find Christian McCaffrey across the middle who took it 74 yards for a score in the second quarter.

In the third quarter, the Niners drew up an end around play that saw Deebo Samuel Sr. out in the open field with Purdy as a lead blocker. He did just enough for Samuel to flip into a 70-yard touchdown.

Christian McCaffrey is one of the best dual threat running backs of all-time. He showed why he's a cheat code against the Cowboys. He had three total touchdowns. On the ground, he went for 13 carries, 62 yards and a touchdown. McCaffrey had a career-high 161 receiving yards, including a 74-yard score which was the longest catch-and-run in his career.

He also scored his eighth consecutive rushing touchdown, passing Joe Dorsey's record that spanned across the 1953-54 seasons. He's been a valuable asset to the Niners offense providing them with a dependable touchdown machine out the backfield.

Niners rookie Kaelon Black is making the most of his touches out the backfield sharing carries with McCaffrey. Black had a solid outing against the Cowboys going 11 carries, 53 yards and a touchdown.

His 25-yard rushing touchdown was arguably the run of the game, maybe the week. Black found the endzone but not before running through a couple of Cowboys defenders along the way. It was the type of smash mouth football that you love to see from your second-string back, let alone a rookie.

He has 79 carries for 356 yards and two touchdowns in his first season. He averages 4.5 yards per carry.

When it comes to some of the best fans, and well-traveled many think of the Raiders, Cowboys, Steelers and Packers. However, you have to mention the 49ers' fanbase, at least you do after how they showed up in Arlington, Texas. Members of the FOX broadcast mentioned how loud, and present, the 49ers fans were at AT&T Stadium. It sounded like they were playing at Levi's Stadium.

If there's any place of concern after the Niners' win against the Cowboys, it's in their secondary. The caveat is that their starting cornerbacks were questionable to play going into the game. However, both stuck it out. Renardo Green finished with seven total tackles while Deommodore Lenoir had five tackles and four pass deflections.

Cowboys quarterback Dak Prescott threw for 343 yards, but wide receiver George Pickens went off for 11 catches, 145 yards and three touchdowns. CeeDee Lamb had a rather quiet game, but still effective with seven catches, 72 yards and a touchdown.

Lenoir took responsibility after the game saying, "I got to play better. They paid me to make plays."

The Cowboys defense took the biggest "L" of the day. They were historically bad, allowing the 49ers to go for 584 total yards. It was the most yards allowed at AT&T Stadium. The 49ers scored three touchdowns of at least 60 yards from scrimmage, which tied for the most by a team in a game over the last 50 years, according to the Elias Sports Bureau.

#2702. Oliver Kahn fires back at Bayern over Musiala: "Loyalty is not a contractual obligation!"

Oliver Kahn has taken aim at Bayern Munich's handling of Jamal Musiala's health situation, delivering a strong message about loyalty, responsibility and player welfare. Following Uli Hoeneß's recent doubts about whether the 23-year-old can ever return to his previous level, the former Bayern CEO has offered a different perspective.

"Contractual loyalty is not loyalty. It is an obligation," Kahn wrote in a LinkedIn post. He argued that a club's true commitment only becomes apparent when a player can no longer deliver on the pitch.

"Clubs like to call themselves families. The problem starts when they talk about family as long as it costs nothing, and switch to talking numbers as soon as it becomes expensive," the 57-year-old added.

Kahn went even further, insisting that fulfilling a contract should not be celebrated as an extraordinary gesture of goodwill. "A club should not pretend that honouring the contract it signed itself is an act of particular humanity. The club did not just buy the performance. It also bought the risk."

The former goalkeeper also drew parallels with Manuel Neuer's serious leg injury in December 2022. Bayern publicly stood behind their captain before signing Yann Sommer just six weeks later.

"Both messages reach the player. But the second one is always heard much louder," Kahn concluded.

Musiala remains under contract until 2030, but has endured a difficult period with injuries and neurological issues. Bayern have stressed that continuing his playing career remains medically justifiable.

#2701. Jaxon Smith-Njigba should be taken seriously as an MVP candidate

As trophy typos go, it’s one thing to have a mistake on “offensive player of the year.” A three-letter award is harder to screw up.

And, frankly, Seahawks receiver Jaxon Smith-Njigba should be taken very seriously as an MVP candidate.

Sure, no receiver has ever won the NFL MVP award. No receiver has ever done what he’s doing, either.

With 171 yards and a touchdown on Sunday against the Raiders, Smith-Njigba has 1,206 receiving yards and 10 touchdowns through 10 games. No one has done that since 1960.

Think about that. Think of all the great receivers who have played since the NFL relaxed the rules to promote more passing. Even then, only three players had ever gotten to 1,200 receiving yards and 10 touchdowns in 10 games before today: Don Hutson in 1942, Elroy “Crazy Legs” Hirsch in 1951, and Raymond Berry in 1960.

Smith-Njigba remains on pace to set the new single-season receiving yardage record. And to become the first player in league history with 2,000 or more receiving yards in a season.

Often, a receiver who has a special season loses MVP votes to the quarterback who fed him the passes. With the Seahawks using both Sam Darnold and Drew Lock, neither will be taking MVP votes away from anyone.

Then there’s the official definition of MVP: “The player who was most valuable to their team over the course of the season.” Smith-Njigba accounts for more than half of the Seattle passing offense. Even when everyone knows the ball is coming to him, he still makes plays.

For now, those inclined to wager on the MVP winner haven’t taken JSN seriously. His odds to win the award are 80-1.

That should change. Because JSN should be regarded as a very real threat to win the MVP award.

#2700. A Win? In This Economy? Canucks Defeat Golden Knights, Snap Seven-Game Skid

The Vancouver Canucks cashed out of Sin City with a surprise haul, defeating the Vegas Golden Knights, 6-3. Was it a Picasso? No, but it did snap a seven-game losing streak and was a much-needed boost for a team down in the dumps. It also marked the first win for the Canucks at T-Mobile Arena since Mar. 7, 2024.

Despite Kevin Lankinen doing the starter's routine in morning skate, Leevi Meriläinen got the start again. He has started eight of the last nine games. He was excellent in this game, making 34 saves on 37 shots. Nikita Tolopilo was called up this morning, and he is expected to start Monday night against the Utah Mammoth.

It was the debut of the Vegas Hometown Remix jerseys on Sunday night. A not-so-subtle casino-themed fit that was reflected in Vegas' play. They played out like a roulette wheel, with a result as unpredictable as ever.

Vegas had a strong start, outshooting the Canucks 5-0 in the first five minutes. However, against the run of play, Elias Pettersson scored a power-play goal at 5:20 to open the scoring. It was on the Canucks' first shot. Carter Hart was completely out of position after a scramble in front of the net.

#2699. Joe Burrow matches Peyton Manning's record for consecutive games with at least four TDs

Joe Burrow has accomplished something that had not been done for over 20 years.

With his 4-yard touchdown to Chase Brown in the third quarter, Burrow became the first since Peyton Manning in 2004 to have at least four total touchdowns in five consecutive games, which is the longest streak in NFL history.

That score gave the Bengals a commanding 31-3 lead with 4:12 left in the third period.

Burrow has been excellent all season, but that's particularly been the case on Sunday night. So far, he's completed 32-of-41 passes for 359 yards with four TDs.

The one difference between Manning's streak and Burrow's is that Manning did it with all passing touchdowns. Between Weeks 8 and 12 in 2004, Manning threw for five, four, five, four, and six TDs with the Colts.

Manning would win his second MVP award for his 2004 season.

Burrow has thrown for four, four, five, three, and now four in his last six games. He's also had a rushing touchdown in each of the last two weeks before Sunday's matchup against Pittsburgh.

#2698. Seahawks shut down Raiders in second half of 21-13 win

Raiders quarterback Fernando Mendoza’s first NFL start started with a bang, but the Seahawks slammed the door on him in the second half on Sunday.

Mendoza ran for a touchdown to open the game helped the Raiders to a 10-7 lead over the defending champions through 30 minutes of play in Las Vegas. The second half was a very different story, however.

The Raiders were shut out until a Matt Gay field goal with 2:04 left to play in the game and the Seahawks scored a pair of touchdowns to pick up a 21-13 win. The victory pushes their record to 6-4 as they head into their bye week and it came after one of the smartest special teams plays we’re likely to see this season.

After Gay’s field goal, Seahawks returner Tory Horton jumped to bat the ensuing kickoff back in bounds rather than let it sail out for a touchback. He also had the presence of mind to tell Rashid Shaheed to return the ball since he stepped out of bounds and couldn’t do it himself. The play wound up taking the clock through the two-minute warning, which meant the Raiders could only stop the clock twice rather than three times.

The Seahawks only ran 17 offensive plays in the first half of the game, but they found their footing behind Zach Charbonnet and Jaxon Smith-Njigba in the second half. Charbonnet ran 12 times for 55 yards and Smith-Njigba continued his remarkable season with seven catches for 160 yards after the break. One of those was a 71-yard touchdown from Drew Lock.

All in all, Seattle picked up 274 yards in the final two quarters while holding the Raiders to 27 yards on their final six possessions. They sacked Mendoza four times in the second half and the loss of tight end Brock Bowers to an ankle injury left the rookie without real options in the passing game.

Mendoza, who finished 17-of-29 for 143 yards, will now try for his first win in Denver next weekend.

#2697. JJ Redick not pleased after Lakers nearly blow 31-point lead to Suns

It was a "disservice" for the Lakers to nearly blow what appeared to be an insurmountable 31-point lead. He said that playing "to our standards" is what's expected from the Lakers — and seeing a huge lead sliced to five points didn't reflect that.

Yes, the Lakers are tied for the best record in the NBA at 12-2. Yes, they have won eight straight games. And yes, his team has one of the best offensive engines in the league in Luka Doncic.

Even with all that and a 131-123 win over the Phoenix Suns, Lakers coach JJ Redick wasn't pleased with what he saw in the fourth quarter Sunday.

Read more: While tied for NBA's best record, Lakers believe they can be better

"We're working too hard and we're playing too hard to build these leads," Redick said. "It's a disservice to each other to take your foot off the gas. You have to play for 48 minutes and we're going to need our defense to win us games. ... Gotta be better in the fourth quarter."

Behind Doncic's sizzling first quarter that led to 43 points, the Lakers opened a 101-70 lead with 1:47 left in the third quarter.

Then came a letdown by the Lakers amid an onslaught from the Suns.

The Suns scored 43 points in the fourth, shot 50% from the field and pulled to within 126-121 with 1:05 left on a layup by Collin Gillespie, who had 13 of his 26 points in the final frame.

Matisse Thybulle of the Lakers blocks the shot of Devin Booker in the first half Sunday. (Kelsey Grant / Getty Images)

The Lakers have been down this road before, losing to Golden State after blowing a 25-point lead.

"You go up 30, it's easy to be like, 'You know, the game is over. They're not going to come back.' But it's very easy in the NBA for teams to come back and make runs because this is the game of runs," Jake LaRavia said. "So again, we just have to do a better job at staying in it for the full 48 minutes and being able to complete a full game."

It took two big plays from Quentin Grimes for the Lakers to not blow the game.

Grimes collected an offensive rebound after LaRavia missed a three-pointer and then pulled up and banked in a three-pointer for a 129-121 Lakers lead with 33.7 seconds left.

Grimes had 22 points on seven-for-10 shooting, going five for six on three-pointers. After missing the previous two games with a right finger injury, he saved the Lakers.

Read more: Lakers' Austin Reaves considered day-to-day with heel injury

He was asked if he called "bank" on his final three.

"I called game. I called game. I called game," said Grimes, smiling. "No, I mean, the bank ain't open on Sunday. I called game. Trying to get out of here and get back to L.A."

Doncic had 20 points in the first quarter, going five for 10 from the field. He leads the NBA in first-quarter scoring (13.6) and in scoring per game (38.6). It was just another masterpiece for Doncic, who was serenaded with "MVP, MVP" chants twice from some fans.

"It was very nice," Doncic said. "I think everybody can see my smile at the free-throw line. And so, that made me happy, honestly."

The Lakers entered the game with three days off after road wins over Detroit, Atlanta and Milwaukee.

"I don't think we should put a ceiling on this group," Redick said. "This group is committed to getting better every single day, recognizing that this is a process to become a high-level team, and that's what I want from this group ... hold each other to that standard every single time.

"And, honestly, that's the frustrating part at times because you see how good we can be. There's no reason to ever play less than that standard."

Lakers guard Austin Reaves missed the game with a left heel contusion/bone bruise.

#2696. Jadarian Price puts Seahawks up 14-10 in third quarter

Fernando Mendoza isn’t the only rookie who has scored his first NFL touchdown in Las Vegas on Sunday.

Seahawks running back Jadarian Price went far later in the first round than Mendoza, but he also found his way into the end zone on Sunday. Price’s 12-yard run put the Seahawks up over the Raiders 14-10 with 9:28 to play in the third quarter.

Price’s touchdown capped a drive that saw Drew Lock hook up with wide receiver Jaxon Smith-Njigba twice for 53 yards. Smith-Njigba only had one catch in the first half and the Seahawks had five first downs on the drive after picking up four of them in the first 30 minutes.

The change in offensive fortunes for the Seahawks means Mendoza will need to mount a comeback to get a win in his first NFL start.

#2695. Mahomes throws five touchdowns as Chiefs thrash Falcons

Patrick Mahomes proved he is back to his imperious best with five touchdowns and 348 passing yards as the Kansas City Chiefs thrashed the Falcons 35-13 at Atlanta on Sunday.

In the three-time Super Bowl champion quarterback's most impressive showing since a nine-month injury absence with torn knee ligaments, Mahomes completed 22-of-35 passes with zero interceptions and one sack.

Victory took the Chiefs level with the idle Denver Broncos at the top of the AFC West, on 7-2.

Rookie Emmett Johnson twice found the end zone, while Josh Downs had a touchdown on his Kansas City debut following a deadline day trade from Indianapolis.

Cyrus Allen and Andrew Armstrong each scored their first Chiefs touchdowns, grabbing passes of 39 yards and 43 yards respectively.

"It just showcases another level to our offense, that we can continue to get better and better," said Mahomes.

Johnson -- stepping up for Kenneth Walker III, who was absent with a groin injury -- also led the Chiefs' running game, with 22 carries for 101 yards.

"I thought we did a great job picking up, obviously Kenneth not being here. I thought Emmett did a great job," said Mahomes.

It was another miserable day for the Falcons, who trailed 28-3 before Bijan Robinson's fourth-quarter touchdown, and have lost five of their past six.

San Francisco beat the Dallas Cowboys 46-40 in a high-scoring, back-and-forth duel.

The 49ers had moved 19 points clear in the second half at Dallas, as Deebo Samuel scored a 70-yard touchdown from a trick play and Christian McCaffrey added another.

Cowboys quarterback Dak Prescott rallied his team with two touchdowns, before McCaffrey took a Brock Purdy pass 66 yards to seemingly put the game to bed.

But CeeDee Lamb grabbed another touchdown in the final minute and Dallas converted the two-point attempt, to ensure a nervy finish.

Victory moved San Francisco to a joint NFL-best 8-1, along with Buffalo who beat the misfiring New York Jets 31-12.

Returning to the Minnesota Vikings on trade deadline day Tuesday, Kirk Cousins threw for two touchdowns to beat divisional rivals Green Bay 30-10.

Cousins spent six successful seasons with the Vikings but tore his Achilles tendon at the Packers' Lambeau Field in 2023, and subsequently left for stints at Atlanta and Las Vegas before returning this week.

"It was emotional, coming to the stadium three years ago, tearing my Achilles here... it's been a tough three years," said Cousins.

The Cleveland Browns rallied from 17-0 down to stun the Houston Texans 27-24, as Quinshon Judkins ran in a touchdown with less than a minute remaining.

Houston led with C.J. Stroud's first-half passing touchdowns to Nico Collins and Xavier Hutchinson, but allowed Cleveland two touchdowns and two field goals without reply.

The Texans thought they had salvaged the win with a Dalton Schultz touchdown, before Watson led yet another late game-winning drive with 53 seconds left.

"Personally for me it was definitely special," said Watson, who was drafted by Houston in 2017 and traded to Cleveland five years later.

The Detroit Lions thrashed New England 35-10 in Munich, Germany.

Patriots quarterback Drake Maye left the field with an ankle injury following a sack by Aidan Hutchinson -- who suffered a knee injury celebrating the sack.

Jahmyr Gibbs had 153 rushing yards with two touchdowns, and another 99 receiving yards, in the Lions' fifth straight win.

The Carolina Panthers needed a field goal with two seconds remaining to beat their divisional rival New Orleans Saints 20-17 and move top of the NFC South.

Indianapolis snapped their five-game losing streak with a 24-22 win over Miami.

Super Bowl champions Seattle beat Las Vegas 21-13, ensuring a frustrating debut start for Raiders quarterback Fernando Mendoza. The top draft pick was sacked six times, but opened the scoring with his first NFL touchdown.

In Sunday's final game, Cincinnati stormed to a fifth straight win, crushing Pittsburgh 38-18, with another four touchdowns and 409 yards for in-form Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow.

#2694. Lakers survive Suns’ furious rally behind Luka Doncic’s 43-point night

PHOENIX — The 21-page presentation toward the beginning of their three-day break from games came with a simple message: Even though the Lakers were tied for the best record and had the league's best offense, they could be even better.

Despite being without Austin Reaves as he allows the bruise in his left heel to recover, the Lakers showed for most of their 131-123 victory over the Suns Sunday afternoon what their better could look like.

Luka Dončić #77 of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts during an NBA game against the Phoenix Suns in Phoenix, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

They also showed more progress needs to be made after they nearly blew a 31-point second-half lead.

The Lakers rode the momentum of another hot start from Luka Dončić (43 points, nine assists) and precise execution to their eighth straight victory to put them at a league-best 12-2 on the season.

Luka Dončić (77) of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts during an NBA game against the Phoenix Suns on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026, in Phoenix. Getty Images

Dončić had 20 points of his scoring total in the first quarter, which was not only his second 20-point opening quarter of the season but also made him the only player in the league with multiple 20-point first quarters.

And after the non-Dončić minutes had been a sore spot for the Lakers for most of the season, they flipped the script on Sunday.

They won the minutes Dončić was on the bench in the second quarter, helping them take a 64-54 lead going into halftime, 101-70 lead late in the third and 104-80 lead going into the fourth.

But the Lakers didn't find the same success when Dončić wasn't in the game to start the fourth.

By the time the Slovenian star subbed back in with 6:48 remaining, the Suns had the momentum after trimming their deficit to 14 points, and eventually six points multiple times late in the fourth.

Luka Dončić #77 of the Los Angeles Lakers controls the ball against Jordan Goodwin #23 of the Phoenix Suns in Phoenix on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. Getty Images

Dončić's late-game offensive heroics, in addition to the Suns missing three free throws in the final two minutes, ensured the Lakers didn't relinquish the lead and held on for the wire-to-wire victory.

Quentin Grimes (22 points) came up with an offensive rebound and banked in a 3-pointer with 34 seconds left to ice the game for the Lakers.

Walker Kessler (14 points, 12 rebounds), Matisse Thybulle (12 points) and Jake LaRavia (12 points) all scored at least 12 points, putting all of the Lakers' starters in double figures in scoring.

Collin Sexton added 16 points and three assists off the bench.
